What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because I just can't seem to keep my focus on anything long enough to actually finish it lately. It's getting really bad, even with the important stuff. Like at work, I'll be trying to trace a network issue for a client, and maybe ten minutes will go by, and I'll realize I've just been staring at the same log entries. I'll read them three times over and still have no idea what they actually say. It's like my brain just checks out.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it's frustrating that I can't seem to get my work done, but I wouldn't say I feel sad or down. Just... annoyed with myself, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. It's actually a bit of a problem. When I'm sitting with a client, or even just in a chess club meeting, my leg is usually bouncing. Or I'm clicking a pen, or... you know, twisting a cable tie around in my fingers. I don't even realize I'm doing it until somebody finally gives me that look, like I'm driving them crazy. I just have a really hard time sitting completely still.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I do that. I mean, I might get distracted or fidgety, but I don't really do things without thinking them through first. I usually think things out... maybe even overthink them sometimes, especially with work stuff. But no, acting on impulse isn't really an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention in your free time, like when you're reading or watching a movie?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. Quiet things that should relax me—like reading a manual, or sitting through a movie with my wife, or even trying one of those breathing exercises—they usually only last a few minutes. After that, I'm... well, I start muttering comments, checking my phone, or I just end up getting up for no real reason. I just can't seem to settle into it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention in all situations, or is it mostly at work or when doing quiet activities?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, honestly. I mean, you asked about work and quiet stuff, and I definitely struggle there. But... is it all situations? I haven't really thought about it that way. I know it happens a lot, but I'd have to really sit and think if there's ever a time where I'm completely focused without any issues.\n\nDoctor: Is there any particular situation or activity where you can focus and pay attention without any difficulty?\n\nPatient: Honestly... no. I'm trying to think of one, but I really can't. Even if it's something I actually want to be doing, my mind still wanders off or I lose my place. So, no, I don't think there's any situation where paying attention is just easy for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble focusing and paying attention, even as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's pretty much always been this way. I was always blurting out answers in class, and I'd lose my homework... even when I had actually finished it. So, yeah, it's been a problem since I was a kid.\n\nDoctor: Have your focus and attention problems caused you any significant problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they definitely have. Since I lost the structure of my old job, things have gotten pretty bad. My professional reputation has taken a real hit. I've lost several freelance clients because I just couldn't keep things organized, and I actually missed out on a senior service-manager role I was up for. People just don't trust me to follow through on things anymore. It's caused a lot of stress at home, too. My wife and I have had to worry about money a lot lately because of all this. So, yeah, it's causing some major problems.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But without that structure, I just... I can't keep it together. My days just turn into these massive piles of sticky notes, half-finished emails, reminders for my chess club, orders for computer parts... it's just a mess everywhere. And I honestly can't tell what needs to happen first. I usually don't figure out what my priority was supposed to be until something is already overdue and I'm scrambling to fix it.

Quiet things that should relax me—like reading a manual, or sitting through a movie with my wife, or even trying one of those breathing exercises—they usually only last a few minutes. After that, I'm... well, I start muttering comments, checking my phone, or I just end up getting up for no real reason. I just can't seem to settle into it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention in all situations, or is it mostly at work or when doing quiet activities?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, honestly. I mean, you asked about work and quiet stuff, and I definitely struggle there. But... is it all situations? I haven't really thought about it that way. I know it happens a lot, but I'd have to really sit and think if there's ever a time where I'm completely focused without any issues.\n\nDoctor: Is there any particular situation or activity where you can focus and pay attention without any difficulty?\n\nPatient: Honestly... no. I'm trying to think of one, but I really can't. Even if it's something I actually want to be doing, my mind still wanders off or I lose my place. So, no, I don't think there's any situation where paying attention is just easy for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble focusing and paying attention, even as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's pretty much always been this way. My mother actually still jokes about it. She says that back in grade school, my report cards always said the same thing—that I was bright, but I never stayed in my seat. I was always blurting out answers in class, and I'd lose my homework... even when I had actually finished it. So, yeah, it's been a problem since I was a kid.\n\nDoctor: Have your focus and attention problems caused you any significant problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they definitely have. Since I lost the structure of my old job, things have gotten pretty bad. My professional reputation has taken a real hit. I've lost several freelance clients because I just couldn't keep things organized, and I actually missed out on a senior service-manager role I was up for. People just don't trust me to follow through on things anymore. It's caused a lot of stress at home, too. My wife and I have had to worry about money a lot lately because of all this. So, yeah, it's causing some major problems.\n\nDoctor: Did you exhibit excessive running, climbing, or other physically active behavior as a child?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I know I just said I couldn't stay in my seat at school, but I wasn't really one of those kids who was constantly running around or climbing on the furniture. It was more just being restless and fidgety, I guess, not really running wild everywhere.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty with organization or time management?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. It's... it's gotten really bad lately. When I had my old job, there was a ticketing system and an escalation calendar, and that kept me on track. But without that structure, I just... I can't keep it together. My days just turn into these massive piles of sticky notes, half-finished emails, reminders for my chess club, orders for computer parts... it's just a mess everywhere. And I honestly can't tell what needs to happen first. I usually don't figure out what my priority was supposed to be until something is already overdue and I'm scrambling to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Did you often feel overly energetic or have trouble calming down as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, and honestly... it's still like that now. I just have a really hard time calming down. Like, on my days off, I always tell myself I'll just rest and take it easy. But I can't. I end up bouncing from a laptop repair, to fixing the garage shelves, to answering a chess email, and then reorganizing some cables. I'm constantly doing things all day, but by night... nothing is ever fully done.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble managing your emotions, like getting easily upset or angry?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I don't really get easily upset or angry. I mean, I get frustrated sometimes when I'm overwhelmed with all the half-finished projects, but I don't have sudden outbursts or anything like that. My emotions are pretty stable, it's just my focus and energy that are all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other symptoms that you haven't mentioned yet, that you think might be related to your focus and attention problems?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess there is one other thing. It's... well, it's the mistakes. I can know a system inside out, but I still manage to miss these stupid little details. It's really frustrating because I know the work. I mean, I might get distracted or fidgety, but I don't really do things without thinking them through first. I usually think things out... maybe even overthink them sometimes, especially with work stuff. But no, acting on impulse isn't really an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention in your free time, like when you're reading or watching a movie?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. Quiet things that should relax me—like reading a manual, or sitting through a movie with my wife, or even trying one of those breathing exercises—they usually only last a few minutes. After that, I'm... well, I start muttering comments, checking my phone, or I just end up getting up for no real reason. I just can't seem to settle into it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention in all situations, or is it mostly at work or when doing quiet activities?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, honestly. I mean, you asked about work and quiet stuff, and I definitely struggle there. But... is it all situations? I haven't really thought about it that way. I know it happens a lot, but I'd have to really sit and think if there's ever a time where I'm completely focused without any issues.\n\nDoctor: Is there any particular situation or activity where you can focus and pay attention without any difficulty?\n\nPatient: Honestly... no. I'm trying to think of one, but I really can't. Even if it's something I actually want to be doing, my mind still wanders off or I lose my place. So, no, I don't think there's any situation where paying attention is just easy for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble focusing and paying attention, even as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's pretty much always been this way. My mother actually still jokes about it. She says that back in grade school, my report cards always said the same thing—that I was bright, but I never stayed in my seat. I was always blurting out answers in class, and I'd lose my homework... even when I had actually finished it. So, yeah, it's been a problem since I was a kid.\n\nDoctor: Have your focus and attention problems caused you any significant problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they definitely have. Since I lost the structure of my old job, things have gotten pretty bad. My professional reputation has taken a real hit. I've lost several freelance clients because I just couldn't keep things organized, and I actually missed out on a senior service-manager role I was up for. People just don't trust me to follow through on things anymore. It's caused a lot of stress at home, too. My wife and I have had to worry about money a lot lately because of all this. So, yeah, it's causing some major problems.\n\nDoctor: Did you exhibit excessive running, climbing, or other physically active behavior as a child?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I know I just said I couldn't stay in my seat at school, but I wasn't really one of those kids who was constantly running around or climbing on the furniture. It was more just being restless and fidgety, I guess, not really running wild everywhere.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty with organization or time management?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. It's... it's gotten really bad lately. When I had my old job, there was a ticketing system and an escalation calendar, and that kept me on track. But without that structure, I just... I can't keep it together. My days just turn into these massive piles of sticky notes, half-finished emails, reminders for my chess club, orders for computer parts... it's just a mess everywhere. And I honestly can't tell what needs to happen first. I usually don't figure out what my priority was supposed to be until something is already overdue and I'm scrambling to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Did you often feel overly energetic or have trouble calming down as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, and honestly... it's still like that now. I just have a really hard time calming down. Like, on my days off, I always tell myself I'll just rest and take it easy. But I can't. I end up bouncing from a laptop repair, to fixing the garage shelves, to answering a chess email, and then reorganizing some cables. I'm constantly doing things all day, but by night... nothing is ever fully done.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble managing your emotions, like getting easily upset or angry?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I don't really get easily upset or angry. I mean, I get frustrated sometimes when I'm overwhelmed with all the half-finished projects, but I don't have sudden outbursts or anything like that. My emotions are pretty stable, it's just my focus and energy that are all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other symptoms that you haven't mentioned yet, that you think might be related to your focus and attention problems?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess there is one other thing. It's... well, it's the mistakes. I can know a system inside out, but I still manage to miss these stupid little details. Like, I'll be doing a router setup and just completely skip one line, or I'll send a client a quote and realize later I put in the wrong serial number. It's really frustrating because I know the work. But lately, those slips have been happening more, and it's really starting to cost me callbacks and trust with my clients.\n\nDoctor: Have your problems with focus, attention, and organization caused significant distress or problems in your daily life beyond what you've already mentioned?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. My wife and I have had to start worrying about money, which is tough, all because people are losing confidence in my ability to just... get the job done right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty with planning and initiating tasks, even when you want to do them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really think so. I mean, I don't have a problem getting started on things. I can plan out a whole IT setup and dive right in without much trouble. Quiet things that should relax me—like reading a manual, or sitting through a movie with my wife, or even trying one of those breathing exercises—they usually only last a few minutes. After that, I'm... well, I start muttering comments, checking my phone, or I just end up getting up for no real reason. I just can't seem to settle into it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention in all situations, or is it mostly at work or when doing quiet activities?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, honestly. I mean, you asked about work and quiet stuff, and I definitely struggle there. But... is it all situations? I haven't really thought about it that way. I know it happens a lot, but I'd have to really sit and think if there's ever a time where I'm completely focused without any issues.\n\nDoctor: Is there any particular situation or activity where you can focus and pay attention without any difficulty?\n\nPatient: Honestly... no. I'm trying to think of one, but I really can't. Even if it's something I actually want to be doing, my mind still wanders off or I lose my place. So, no, I don't think there's any situation where paying attention is just easy for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble focusing and paying attention, even as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's pretty much always been this way. My mother actually still jokes about it. She says that back in grade school, my report cards always said the same thing—that I was bright, but I never stayed in my seat. I was always blurting out answers in class, and I'd lose my homework... even when I had actually finished it. So, yeah, it's been a problem since I was a kid.\n\nDoctor: Have your focus and attention problems caused you any significant problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they definitely have. Since I lost the structure of my old job, things have gotten pretty bad. My professional reputation has taken a real hit. I've lost several freelance clients because I just couldn't keep things organized, and I actually missed out on a senior service-manager role I was up for. People just don't trust me to follow through on things anymore. It's caused a lot of stress at home, too. My wife and I have had to worry about money a lot lately because of all this. So, yeah, it's causing some major problems.\n\nDoctor: Did you exhibit excessive running, climbing, or other physically active behavior as a child?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I know I just said I couldn't stay in my seat at school, but I wasn't really one of those kids who was constantly running around or climbing on the furniture. It was more just being restless and fidgety, I guess, not really running wild everywhere.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty with organization or time management?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. It's... it's gotten really bad lately. When I had my old job, there was a ticketing system and an escalation calendar, and that kept me on track. But without that structure, I just... I can't keep it together. My days just turn into these massive piles of sticky notes, half-finished emails, reminders for my chess club, orders for computer parts... it's just a mess everywhere. And I honestly can't tell what needs to happen first. I usually don't figure out what my priority was supposed to be until something is already overdue and I'm scrambling to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Did you often feel overly energetic or have trouble calming down as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, and honestly... it's still like that now. I just have a really hard time calming down. Like, on my days off, I always tell myself I'll just rest and take it easy. But I can't. I end up bouncing from a laptop repair, to fixing the garage shelves, to answering a chess email, and then reorganizing some cables. I'm constantly doing things all day, but by night... nothing is ever fully done.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble managing your emotions, like getting easily upset or angry?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I don't really get easily upset or angry. I mean, I get frustrated sometimes when I'm overwhelmed with all the half-finished projects, but I don't have sudden outbursts or anything like that. My emotions are pretty stable, it's just my focus and energy that are all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other symptoms that you haven't mentioned yet, that you think might be related to your focus and attention problems?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess there is one other thing. It's... well, it's the mistakes. I can know a system inside out, but I still manage to miss these stupid little details. Like, I'll be doing a router setup and just completely skip one line, or I'll send a client a quote and realize later I put in the wrong serial number. It's really frustrating because I know the work. But lately, those slips have been happening more, and it's really starting to cost me callbacks and trust with my clients.\n\nDoctor: Have your problems with focus, attention, and organization caused significant distress or problems in your daily life beyond what you've already mentioned?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. It's... it's been pretty bad lately. Since I lost the structure of my old job, things have kind of fallen apart. My reputation has taken a real hit. I've actually lost a few freelance clients recently because of all those little mistakes and missed details I mentioned. \n\nAnd it's not just the freelance stuff. I was up for a senior service-manager role, and I missed out on it because they just didn't trust me to follow through and keep things organized. It's stressful. My wife and I have had to start worrying about money, which is tough, all because people are losing confidence in my ability to just... get the job done right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty with planning and initiating tasks, even when you want to do them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really think so. I mean, I don't have a problem getting started on things. I can plan out a whole IT setup and dive right in without much trouble. The initiating part is usually fine. \n\nIt's... well, like I said before, it's staying focused enough to actually finish the job, or not messing up the details along the way. But just getting the ball rolling? No, I haven't really noticed any issue with that.\n\nDoctor: Have your focus and attention problems caused any significant problems in your personal relationships, such as with your wife or friends?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my wife is definitely stressed about the money situation, like I mentioned, but that's more about the financial strain than my focus. We still get along fine, and my friendships are pretty solid. It's really just the work stuff where this is causing a major problem.\n\nDoctor: Did you often interrupt others or blurt out answers as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. My mother actually still jokes about it. She says that back in grade school, my report cards always said the same thing—that I was bright, but I never stayed in my seat and I always blurted out answers before the teacher finished asking the question. And I'd always lose my homework, even when I had actually done it. So, yeah, that was definitely a thing when I was a kid.\n\nDoctor: Did you often feel like you were 'on the go' or like you couldn't slow down as a child?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I know I had trouble staying in my seat at school, like I said, but I don't really remember feeling like I couldn't slow down or that I was constantly on the go. It was more just being easily distracted and blurting things out. I don't recall feeling driven by a motor or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still now, as an adult?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's actually pretty constant. Even when I'm in a meeting or just supposed to sit still and listen to someone, it feels like there's this motor humming under my skin. I just get this overwhelming urge that I need to stand up, check something, or just move on to the next problem right that second. It's hard to just be still.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
